**This is the ONLY open-work list** (owner, 2026-09-12). Handoff memories, the
audit-bus ledger and agent reports are evidence, not backlogs: anything still
open from them gets a line here, with the check that guards it once one exists.

**Layout (reconciled 2026-09-23, Q16):** (1) the live QUEUE, verbatim, first;
(2) every item still open from older sections, one line each with its current
status and a pointer into the archive; (3) the 2026-09-02 ledger's leftovers
(docs/audit/OPEN_ITEMS.md is retired to a pointer); (4) the audit bus, by
reference. History (closed sections, narratives) is verbatim in
[docs/archive/OPEN-history-2026-09.md](archive/OPEN-history-2026-09.md),
with a reconcile log of every item closed and its evidence. Nothing was deleted.

**Priority among the open queue items (lead's order, 2026-09-23 — money and
security first, then alerting, then the audit, then hygiene):** Q53 (DB outage
root cause), Q50 (card holds on cancelled jobs, Stripe side), Q3 (Stripe test
balance + monitor), Q30 (missed daily crons never catch up), Q2 (test runs raise
real alerts), Q42 (open alert-ledger items), Q9 (skipped money/authz review),
Q14 (Supabase security advisors), Q45 (prove a restore), Q58 (one place
for everything open), Q52 (false-green hunt), Q54 (front/back parity), Q35 (exhaustive

  REVIEW FOLLOW-UP 2026-09-23 (migration 20260923050059): HIGH fixed — the
  error_logs trigger's ledger upsert could stall a concurrent caller until its
  COMMIT (measured 3004 ms vs a 3000 ms hold; prod lock_timeout=0). Now
  ops_alert_record bounds the wait to 100 ms and, if the row is busy, queues
  the occurrence in `ops_alert_pending`; ops_alert_verify() folds it in (hourly).
  Measured after: 103 ms / 104 ms (existing row / brand-new fingerprint), both
  transactions commit, caller's lock_timeout untouched
  (scripts/probes/ops-alert-ledger-concurrency.embedded-pg.mjs; class guard
  src/test/errorLogTriggersNeverWait.test.ts, red on the original). MEDIUM
  fixed — normalise v2 keeps [1-5]xx codes after http/status/returned/
  responded/code/error and digits glued to a name (v2, job_7); ids, amounts,
  timestamps, signed numbers still stripped; provable existing rows re-keyed
  (merged where two v1 items are now one; open wins). LOW fixed —
  check_ops_digest_delivery() is ok:false when the ops-daily-digest
  expectation row is missing (row exists live, registered 2026-09-14); the
  side effect is commented at the ops_alert_condition call site.
  lh-silent-failure review of the fix: re-key merge chain lost counts/open
  status (fixed: row re-read per iteration, PGlite chain case red without it);
  one bad pending row stopped every fold (fixed: per-row sub-block, row stays
  queued); 6+ digit numbers were `<id>` (fixed: hex ids need a letter).
  STILL OPEN: (e) no watchdog on `ops_alert_pending` — if the hourly `ledger`
  job stops, queued occurrences never fold and nothing says so (alert on
  oldest queued_at age from something other than that job); the brief
  (ops-alert-ledger.mjs) reads the ledger without folding, so it undercounts
  during a storm.
  One tracked item per distinct alert fingerprint,
  from error_logs (server rows, every severity), Slack posts that bypass

- [ ] **Q2 Test runs raise real alerts.** E2E/seed jobs trigger stuck-payment
  alerts, the ec3428da "refund refused" alert (a test job paid 09-12, no
  dispute, where something keeps retrying a refund), and user-facing
  notification copy posting into #ops-alerts. Fix it where it starts: tests
  clean up their checkouts, detectors handle is_seed deliberately, and alerts
  from E2E jobs go to their own channel or tag. Nothing silently dropped.
  FIXED 2026-09-23 (commit "fix(alerts): test data stops paging", migration
  20260923052520). ROOT CAUSES, measured on prod:
  (a) the relay: send-push-notification mirrors an admin's push to Slack when
  the admin has no push token, and tested only the ROLE. The owner's account
  (76b07824, admin AND a party to seed job 5eed0a20…08) received its own user
  mail — "Did you finish this job?" x5, "Has this job been finished?" x5,
  "We've asked support to step in" x10, "Job auto-cancelled" x4, chat
  messages — and every one posted as a critical page. Now only operator types
  (admin_alert, system_alert) mirror; a seed subject (job=/user= in the link)
  goes to the digest. The stalled-job admin link now names the job: all of a
  day's stalled jobs shared one once-a-day key, so a real one could be
  swallowed by a seed one.
  (b) detectors: error_log_is_seed() (tags.seed or a '-seed' source) keeps
  seed rows out of Slack and the ledger; they stay in error_logs, so the daily
  digest lists them. detect_stuck_payments -> 'detect_stuck_payments-seed'
  for seed jobs/posters; postSlackOpsAlert({seed}) for edge callers
  (stalled-completion passes it). Guard
  src/test/alertingDetectorsDeclareSeedPolicy.test.ts (red on origin/main
  741e9d3a6: detect_stuck_payments + 13 edge files).
  (c) checkouts: every stuck-payment job was an E2E job the spec had already
  CANCELLED; nothing ever expired its Checkout Session or moved it out of
  'unpaid' (void-cancelled-payments' abandon sweep only read status='open').
  Its new Part B2 expires the session and marks cancelled+unpaid jobs
  'abandoned' (real posters who cancel mid-checkout too — the session could
  still be paid for 24h); detect_stuck_payments gives cancelled jobs 2h for it.
  (d) DLQ: all 51 dead letters (09-12/13) were to is_seed mailinator accounts
  and died of `Resend 429 daily_quota_exceeded` — the 09-13 seed-heavy run
  enqueued 319 emails in a day (email_send_log). The DLQ verify now needs a
  later 'sent' row per NON-seed recipient (archiving no longer clears it);
  seed-only DLQs report to the digest. OPEN: whether seed accounts should get
  mail at all (see MORNING QUESTIONS 4).
  The ec3428da "refund refused" item: that job is now completed/released
  (updated 2026-09-22 22:23Z) and no error_logs row mentions it; nothing to

## QUEUE (cont.) — gaps measured 2026-09-23 (owner: "anything at all")

- [~] **Q11 FIXED 2026-09-23 (branch cloud/q275-sentry), re-measure pending: the first hourly prod-errors sync after landing must log `sentry: N issue(s) with an event in the last 25h synced` and close the "sentry alerts are not synced" ledger item. ROOT CAUSE (measured, Sentry activity feed): Sentry itself resolves every helpr-4m issue 1 hour after its last event (`set_resolved_by_age`, age 1, by system) and reopens it on the next (`set_regression`); JAVASCRIPT-1H cycled 20 times 09-11..09-23; 2E/2F/2G were resolved the same way, nobody resolved them. AND the ledger sync never read Sentry at all: it used SENTRY_AUTH_TOKEN (upload token) and got 403 every hour (prod-errors run 35895960717: "Sentry could not be read: Sentry 403"; ledger item "sentry alerts are not synced" open 9x), and asked only `is:unresolved`, which would have missed every auto-resolved issue. CHANGE: scripts/lib/sentryLedgerSync.mjs; the sync reads with SENTRY_READ_TOKEN (prod-errors.yml passes it), takes every issue with an event in the last 25h WHATEVER its Sentry status at its real lastSeen (Sentry status/substatus in sample_ref), and ops_alert_apply reopens a closed item on any newer occurrence (pinned by src/test/pglite/opsAlertLedger.pglite.mjs). GUARD: src/test/sentryLedgerSyncTracksRegressions.test.ts (5 @mutate, all red). OWNER OPTION (not needed for the ledger): Sentry project settings -> Auto Resolve (currently 1 hour) could be turned off so the Sentry UI stops reading "resolved". Was:** Sentry issues are "resolved" while they keep firing. JAVASCRIPT-1H
  "socket closed: 1005": 66 events, 4 users, last seen 1h ago, status resolved.
  The 09-22 profile-timeout issues -> "We couldn't load your account" error
  screen are also resolved with no fix recorded. Find out what resolves them
  (auto-resolve setting? a script?). Feed Sentry into the Q1 ledger; a regression
  must reopen it.

GUARD: src/test/errorSurfacesReport.test.tsx + src/test/pglite/userErrorScreenLedger.pglite.mjs.** 14 days of error_logs: 1 non-seed occurrence, the OWNER's account at 2026-09-22 15:10Z, right after the pg_cron/DB outage window (14:00-15:00Z). The rest were E2E test accounts (09-13, 09-15). Follow-up is Q39. Was: A real user saw "We couldn't load your account" (Sentry 2F/2E/2G, protection built by Q39 (was: PROTECTION NOT YET BUILT).

- [x] **Q13 DONE (2026-09-23): script-src has no `'unsafe-inline'`** in vercel.json,
  index.html's Capacitor meta or public/_headers. Measured inventory (built dist/):
  3 inline scripts in index.html (storage probe, boot watchdog, pre-paint theme),
  1 in offline.html, 1 in tools/apple-jwt.html, 2 JSON-LD data blocks (not
  executed, not governed), and ONE inline handler: the async-CSS
  `onload="this.media='all'"` from vite.config.ts. That handler was the trap:
  dropping 'unsafe-inline' alone left the whole app UNSTYLED (media=print, Times)
  in Chromium and WebKit. It is now a hashed swap `<script>`. Each inline script
  is allowed by its sha256. No runtime inline scripts (MapKit is loaded by
  src=; Stripe is a redirect; PostHog external loading is off). Verified on a
  prod build served with the vercel.json headers, both engines: boot, /login,
  /browse, /dashboard (authed), /profile, MapKit map, Upgrade to Stripe sandbox
  checkout, boot watchdog reload, offline.html retry; a `javascript:` href, a
  script-inserted inline script and an `onerror=` handler are all blocked.
  Guards: `src/test/cspScriptSrc.test.ts` (source) and
  `scripts/check-csp-inline-scripts.mjs` (end of `npm run build` and `build:ios`,
  so Vercel refuses a bundle whose inline script lost its hash).
  Left open: (a) style-src still has `'unsafe-inline'`. React `style=` props and
  the boot shell need it; lower risk (no script execution). (b) Every page logs
  one `script-src eval` violation, and it was already there before this change:
  zod v4's `allowsEval` probe (`Function("")` in try/catch, forms chunk). It is
  harmless, and `z.config({ jitless: true })` would silence it. (c) public/_headers
  is Netlify-format and Vercel ignores it. It was kept in sync, but it is dead config.
  (d) Not device-tested in a native WKWebView. The Capacitor build's meta CSP was
  checked in desktop WebKit over http only.

- [x] **Q154 DONE (2026-09-23): dashboard feed cards overlapped after a job was optimistically removed — VirtualizedJobList cleared its own row-size cache.** Measured on prod as helper-e2e with the funded fixture 1bb36551 (local build, evidence ~/.lh-shots/q154/): before the fix, applying from the job sheet (useApplyFlow onMutate drops the card) moved the row pitch from 110px to 92px at 375 and 114px to 92px at 1440, a 10px card overlap where there had been an 8px/12px gap, unchanged at +40s. Cause: `useEffect(() => virtualizer.measure(), [items.length])` in src/components/dashboard/VirtualizedJobList.tsx. In @tanstack/virtual-core 3.17 `measure()` CLEARS the per-key size cache, and rows that stay mounted are never re-measured (same node, same size: neither the ref callback nor the ResizeObserver fires), so every surviving row fell back to `estimateSize={92}`. Fix: the call is removed; sizes are cached by stable job id, so a new count re-lays the rows out on its own. No style changes. After: pitch 110 (375) / 114 (1440) held at +1.5s, +4s and +40s, gaps intact; before/after screenshots at both widths reviewed and recorded. Both test applications withdrawn (fixture back to 0 applications, open/escrow). **Guard:** src/components/dashboard/VirtualizedJobList.test.tsx renders the real virtualizer, removes an item and asserts every surviving row sits at least one measured row-height below the previous one (1 @mutate re-adding the `measure()` call; red: "expected 92 to be greater than or equal to 110").

- [x] **Q135 A plain whole-repo `npx vitest run` deadlocks on its own gate lock (found by the Q129 lane 2026-09-23).** src/test/gateLockGlobalSetup.ts is the root `globalSetup`, and both projects in vitest.config.ts (`unit`, `tz-sweep`) `extends: true`, so it runs twice in ONE process: the first call takes ~/.lh-gate.lock with this pid, the second finds the owner pid alive (itself) and waits (log printed "acquiring the gate lock" twice, then "waiting for pid 70270" = its own pid; hung 30+ min, killed). `npm run test:unit` / `node scripts/gateLock.mjs -- npx vitest run` are unaffected (LH_GATE_LOCK_HELD=1). Fix: make acquireGateLock re-entrant for `owner.pid === process.pid` (and release only on the last teardown), with a case in src/test/gateLockGlobalSetup.test.ts that calls setup() twice in-process. DONE 2026-09-23 (cloud/q135-q136): scripts/gateLock.mjs acquireGateLock is re-entrant: when owner.pid === process.pid it counts another hold instead of waiting, with the count kept per process on globalThis (each vitest project may load its own copy of the module); releaseGateLock removes the lock only on the LAST release. The lock path is now resolved per call, and LH_GATE_LOCK_PATH overrides it so the test never touches ~/.lh-gate.lock (a worker thread's process.env.HOME does not reach os.homedir()). Guard: src/test/gateLockGlobalSetup.test.ts "setup() twice in-process acquires twice and releases only on the last teardown" + 2 @mutate lines. RED on the old acquire/release logic (origin/main gateLock.mjs, only its path made lazy): "[gate-lock] waiting for pid 1209" = its own pid, then "Gate lock ... held by pid 1209 ... past the wait limit"; RED with early release (lock gone after the first teardown); GREEN 3/3.

- [x] **Q202 DONE 2026-09-23: card-dispute (chargeback) protection, all four owner decisions built.** (1) CLAW BACK: a real chargeback (charge.dispute.created, and the new charge.dispute.funds_withdrawn for an inquiry the bank escalates) on a job whose Helpr was paid reverses the Helpr's transfer(s) in transfer_group job_<id> up to the disputed amount (stripe-webhook/handlers/_chargebackClawback.ts), one chargeback_clawbacks row per (dispute, transfer) claimed before the Stripe call (migration 20260923154149, RLS: admins + the payee read, service role writes), Stripe idempotency keys, and on any resume Stripe is asked first (listReversals / the transfer group) so nothing is reversed or repaid twice past the ~24h key window; the job goes released -> chargeback by CAS before any reversal; a Stripe refusal (e.g. short balance) pages critical via the ops alert ledger, a transient failure 500s for redelivery; WON pays each reversed amount back (idempotent) and records the re-payment in payout_transfers as paid, LOST leaves it reversed; the payee is told in-app each time (role-neutral); transfer.reversed recognises its own clawback and does not freeze. (2) 3D SECURE 'any' on every Checkout Session from $300 (_shared/threeDSecure.ts: create-payment escrow, gift-card shortfall, tip; create-gift-card-checkout); the challenge runs on Stripe's hosted page on web and in the in-app browser sheet on native, and an abandoned challenge (open session, PI requires_action) can be paid again: create-payment expires the session and cancels the PI, refusing if it can no longer be canceled. (3) MAX JOB PRICE $1,000: ONE constant, _shared/jobBudgetLimits.ts, re-exported by src/lib/moneyLimits.ts; create-payment refuses a checkout outside $10-$1,000 before any session exists; migration 20260923154148 sets validate_job_budget() and the jobs_budget_range / jobs_urgent_fee_ceiling CHECKs to 1000 (measured first: 47 rows above $1,000, all is_seed, open/abandoned, no PaymentIntent; they are clamped to 1000, a real row would get NOT VALID instead; PGlite 3x); the post-job budget field says "The most a job can be is $1,000. Split a bigger project into separate jobs." and the step no longer shows Done above the cap (screenshots ~/.lh-shots/q202/, reviews recorded). (4) STANDARD PAY 3 DAYS after the job is marked done: _shared/escrowTiming.ts standardPayoutAtIso (done + 72h, never before now) used by auto-release-payment, create-payment's two-sided release and the re-pay checkout (anchored on the new charge); PAYOUT_HOLD_HOURS 48 after an auto-complete; every sentence that stated the timing now says "3 days after the job is marked done" (notifications, SubmittedStep, Earnings, Help Center, Terms); instant payout unchanged (it moves money already sent to a Helpr's Stripe balance, it does not shorten the 3 days). The admin stalled-job escalation stays at 48h on purpose. GUARDS (each proven red by mutation, 16/16, 5/5, 8/8, 6/6): src/test/edge/chargebackClawback.test.ts, src/test/threeDSecureOnLargeCharges.test.ts, src/test/jobBudgetCapIsOneConstant.test.ts (one constant: client + create-payment + newest DB trigger and CHECKs + seeds), src/test/standardPayoutThreeDays.test.ts. Owner decisions: (1) "CLAW BACK: on charge.dispute.created for an already-released job, reverse the Helpr's transfer(s) for that job (idempotency key per dispute+transfer, ledger row, never double-reverse, handle partial/insufficient-balance errors loudly via the alert ledger). On charge.dispute.closed WON, re-pay the Helpr (idempotent). LOST: stays reversed. Tell the Helpr in-app (role-neutral copy)." (2) "3D SECURE on charges >= $300: request_three_d_secure 'any' (or 'challenge') on those PaymentIntents/Checkout sessions; make sure the client flow handles requires_action on web AND native." (3) "MAX JOB PRICE $1,000: client constant, server validators, the DB CHECK (replay-safe migration; existing rows above $1,000: measure first, only test data expected; do not break them), copy that states the cap." - [x] **Q174 DONE 2026-09-23: every HTTP cron command now records its pg_net request id in cron_http_requests (cron_http_tag), and sweep_cron_http_failures joins on it, so a manual probe is never filed as a cron failure. PGlite proof: 3 applies, 6 FAILs on the old state. Reviewed by lh-silent-failure (26/26 live HTTP crons covered). Guard: src/test/cronHttpRequestsAreTagged.test.ts. Follow-up: Q218. Was:** sweep_cron_http_failures files agents' manual pg_net probes as cron failures (found working Q42, 2026-09-23).** It reads every net._http_response row (pg_net does not record which cron sent a request) and names a body-less timeout by proximity to the nearest HTTP cron. Measured: the 23 Sep 08:00Z "arrival-confirm-reminder … Timeout of 400 ms" row cannot be the cron (its command uses timeout_milliseconds 30000); the "money-reconciliation returned 500" rows at 08:00Z/08:15Z were manual `?include_seed=1` POSTs at 07:52, 07:54 and 08:12Z (function_edge_logs). Both ledger items closed with that evidence. Fix: have cron commands tag their requests (e.g. an `x-cron-job` header or `?cron=<jobname>`) and have the sweeper skip untagged responses, or have probes use a marker; guard red on a planted untagged 500.

- [x] **Q178 DONE 2026-09-23: the current page's own chunk is requested in the FIRST round after the entry, and the likely next pages are warmed once it has loaded.** Measured (scripts/perf/measure-load.mjs: production build, 375, 1.6 Mbps / 150 ms RTT, 4x CPU, cold cache, gzip, median of 3; evidence ~/.lh-shots/q178/gz-base.json vs gz-E.json): / H1 4543 -> 2662 ms; /browse first card 5512 -> 4211 ms; /login form 4537 -> 2696 ms; /signup form 4752 -> 2978 ms. Causes found: vite.config.ts `resolveDependencies: () => []` emptied EVERY preload list (not just index.html's), so each lazy chunk walked its graph one round per level (20 JS rounds before the H1); App.tsx's lazy PageTransition suspended the page behind framer-motion (Index requested only after framer, at 3.9 s); ~45 KB gzip of route skeletons for OTHER routes on every cold load; useAuthReady fetched the 69 KB Sentry SDK mid-load for a breadcrumb it then dropped. Fix: tiny src/entry.ts starts main.tsx and the page chunk (src/boot/routePreload.ts) in the same tick, every dynamic import gets its preload list, preload-helper has its own chunk, PageTransition renders a plain keyed div until the animated one loads after the page settles, skeletons load on demand, cold-launch phases are buffered (src/lib/coldLaunchPhases.ts). Prefetch: src/lib/routePrefetch.ts LIKELY_NEXT_ROUTES (guest /browse /login /signup; signed-in /dashboard /messages /my-jobs /post-job /profile) after load + 1 s network quiet, skipped on Save-Data / 2G. index.html's boot watchdog skips data-lh-page-preload links so a stale PAGE chunk keeps its in-app recovery (e2e/happy-path/stale-deploy.spec.ts + zz-runtime-probe chunk tests: 37 passed locally). Tried and reverted: withholding the page import's preload list (plain import()) to avoid the watchdog: +232 ms on / (2662 -> 2894). GUARD: scripts/perf/critical-path.mjs --check in .github/workflows/bundle-size.yml and npm run gate (budget scripts/perf/critical-path-budget.json, rounds exact both ways, KB band; red on origin/main: page chunk in round 7, ready after 11-12 rounds) + src/test/criticalPathBudget.test.ts (10 @mutate registrations, all killed). Was: Q178 LOAD SPEED: find what the ~4.5s on a slow phone connection is spent on (owner 2026-09-23: 'make sure everything is loading as fast as possible').** Measured (production build, 375, ~1.6Mbps/150ms): /, /browse and /login each take ~4.5s from navigation to real content, identical across routes. The entry bundle is small (index js 12KB gz, css 32KB gz), and starting the route chunk at module load did NOT move the number (4492->4502ms, reverted). So the time is a request WATERFALL (chained modulepreloads/vendor chunks, fonts, auth/session calls, first data fetch). WATERFALL MEASURED 2026-09-23 (landing, 375, throttled): 100 requests before the H1; the landing page's own chunk (Index) is requested only at ~3.8s, LAST. Ahead of it on the critical chain: ~20 route skeletons App.tsx imports statically (Dashboard/Activity/Earnings/Profile skeletons, NotificationPanel, tabs), app-shared 137KB, supabase 53KB (~2.2s), sentry 69KB (~3.5s), framer-motion proxy 38KB, MobileNav/DesktopSidebarNav/dialogs/sheet — in ~8 dependent rounds (0.6, 1.2, 1.4, 1.5, 1.7, 2.0, 2.1, 3.0s...). A preload at App module scope cannot help (App evaluates only after its static graph). Fix: make the shell's static graph minimal (route fallbacks and chrome lazy or per-route), keep sentry/framer/supabase off the landing critical path, and start the entry route's chunk before the shell graph (e.g. main.tsx kicks it off before importing App, or a modulepreload for it). Then re-measure; record a network waterfall per route, cut the chain (parallelise, inline critical, defer non-critical, preconnect/early-hint Supabase), and prove each route's number moves. Guard: a CI performance budget per entry route on the throttled profile.

PGlite proof src/test/pglite/cronCatchUpHttpOutcome.pglite.mjs (29 PASS; red before: 15 assertions FAIL + 2 missing-column errors); cronCatchUp.pglite.mjs now also applies it (ALL PASS). GUARD: src/test/cronHttpRequestsAreTagged.test.ts (Q207/Q218 block, 5 new @mutate, each proven red) + src/test/cronCatchUpPolicy.test.ts (@mutate lines moved to the newest definition, 8/8 red). Parts (1), (3), (4) were fixed by migration 20260923163407 (run_missed_cron_catch_up restated from 20260923145516 with only these changes); part (2) OPEN, waits for Q174. lh-silent-failure REVIEW ONLY of the diff: no HIGH; its MEDIUM (after a caught statement_timeout the rest of the tick ran with no timer and lock_timeout 0, so a later job could hang the tick and its advisory lock) fixed: after a cancel no further job runs that tick (they wait for the next); its LOW (a paused-then-resumed job's first slot looked missed) fixed: a pause restarts 'since'. Other LOWs accepted: first period after deploy has no schedule-table proof (run-at-slot / one-period-earlier proofs still apply); rows for deleted jobids are not pruned. GUARD: src/test/pglite/cronCatchUp.pglite.mjs (reschedule, pause, lock_timeout and query_canceled cases; red on 20260923145516: 15 FAIL; each change also mutated red) + src/test/cronCatchUpPolicy.test.ts (structural, @mutate on the new migration). Was:** Hardening findings of the Q189 lh-silent-failure review of run_missed_cron_catch_up() (migrations 20260923133021 + 20260923145516, policy seed 20260923145117). (1) DONE, MEDIUM: a RESCHEDULED daily/weekly job looked like a missed slot (slot from the current schedule; any earlier run counted as proof), so moving charge-recurring-visits (catch_up=false) to a later time would file a 'cron-missed-slot' error for a day it already ran, and a catch_up=true job would run twice that day. Now a slot needs proof the job was on THIS schedule: a failed run started at the slot, or an earlier run AND (a tick saw it on this schedule at or before the slot: new table cron_catchup_schedules; or a run / catch-up one period earlier). (2) OPEN: record the pg_net request id of a caught-up HTTP job so its HTTP outcome can be checked (today 'caught_up' means the request was queued, not that it succeeded). Waits for Q174, which is changing how cron HTTP requests are tagged. (3) DONE: the tick's 200ms lock_timeout applied to the EXECUTEd job; the job now runs with the session's lock_timeout and 200ms is restored after the block. (4) DONE: EXCEPTION WHEN OTHERS does not catch query_canceled, so a statement_timeout rolled back the whole tick (every claim, retried every 10 min); now caught per command, recorded catch_up_failed and alerted, the tick continues.

- [ ] **Q272 The press sweep's end-of-run profile "restore" races its own shards (found by Q200, 2026-09-23).** press-every-control snapshots each shared account's profile once PER SHARD at start and PATCHes every differing column back at that shard's end (cleanup() in scripts/audit/pressProdSafety.mjs). Four shards run concurrently, so a shard that snapshotted after another shard's press (or after a cancelled run that never restored) writes the wrong value back: run 35837735324 shard 2 at 11:02Z "restored senior_mode, available_until" to the flipped value. Q200 stops toggles reaching the profile, but any other profile column a press can change is still restored from a racy snapshot. Fix: one snapshot per RUN (taken before any shard starts, e.g. a preflight job artifact) and one restore after all shards finish; or restore only columns this shard itself changed. Guard: a test that two interleaved shard snapshots cannot restore a value neither started from.

The stale-chunk recovery (src/lib/chunkReload.ts) should land the visitor on the working page after one reload, never on the error screen. Reproduce by loading a page, deploying, then navigating; guard with a spec that serves a missing chunk.
  - **Status 2026-09-23 (cloud/q199-deploy-stale-chunk, not yet on main):** root cause reproduced locally: a reload that lands on the PREVIOUS build's HTML again (deploy still propagating) finds its chunks gone, and both layers gave up at once: index.html's boot watchdog showed "Helpr couldn't load." for any failure within 10s of a reload, and a route chunk showed "This page hit a problem." for 30s before its second try. A reload that lands on a consistent build always recovered (1 reload). Fix: one shared decision, `decideChunkReload` (src/lib/chunkReload.ts), with an ES5 copy in the watchdog: quiet retries at +0/+5/+15/+40s on one sessionStorage counter, the error screen only after 4 reloads, never a loop; the watchdog also fails closed without sessionStorage (it used to reload forever there). Guards: `e2e/happy-path/deploy-stale-chunk.spec.ts` (real build A→B swap: clean deploy = exactly 1 reload then /support and /legal; propagating = no error screen at any 100ms poll; broken build = error screen after exactly 4 reloads) was 3/5 red on origin/main, 5/5 green with the fix; `src/lib/chunkReload.test.ts` (schedule, cap, and a parity grid holding the watchdog's copy equal to the TypeScript one). `[~]` until landed and re-walked on prod during a real deploy (not measurable from here).

5. **Push notifications (Q82): three things only you can do.** The code
   cause is fixed and pushed (see Q82), but no phone has the fix yet.
   (a) **Cut a TestFlight build from main** (at or after the Q82 fix commit)
   and install it on your iPhone. The only builds with the AppDelegate
   forwarding are ce4d38d59 (2026-09-02) and later, and every build so far
   also has the boot race (it depends on timing, so it may not hit every
   launch). (b) **On that build: sign in, go to Home, tap "Enable" on the
   notifications pill, then Allow.** The pill only shows after you've posted
   or applied, or on a later launch more than an hour after install. If you tapped "Don't
   Allow" on any earlier build, iOS will not ask again: turn it on in
   Settings > Helpr > Notifications first, then cold-launch the app. Within
   seconds `select count(*) from push_tokens` should be 1 and
   analytics_events should show `push_token_saved`. If it doesn't, the
   analytics events say which step failed. (c) **Only if you run the app
   straight from Xcode on your phone:** those builds get SANDBOX APNs tokens,
   and prod sends to the production APNs host (`APNS_USE_SANDBOX` is unset),
   so APNs rejects them with BadDeviceToken and the sender deletes the row.
   TestFlight/App Store builds are unaffected. Leave this alone unless you
   test from Xcode. APNs credentials are fine: APNS_KEY_ID, APNS_TEAM_ID
   (digest matches P85MCK558V), APNS_BUNDLE_ID (digest matches com.Helpr)
   and APNS_AUTH_KEY are all set in Supabase secrets. Whether the .p8 key
   itself is valid can't be checked without a real token to send to.

- [x] **Q107 CredentialsTab saveBusinessName has no `.select()` (Q99 follow-up).**
  `update({business_name})` checked only `error`, so a zero-row update showed
  "Saved." and patched the cache from the request. Fixed: chains
  `.select(SELECT_COLS)` and unwraps through `unwrapMutationRow`
  (src/lib/mutationResult.ts), same pattern the rest of the file uses since
  2a1964cc5; the local cache patch now mirrors the RETURNED row via
  `setFromRow`, not the request. Guard:
  src/test/businessNameSaveObservesRowCount.test.ts (source-scan, 1 @mutate),
  proven red on the unfixed code (missing `.select()` on the business_name
  write), green on the fix. `patchCache` became dead code as a direct result
  of this fix (its only caller was the code this replaced) and the
  `no-unused-vars` pre-commit lint blocked the commit on it, so it was
  removed rather than left; the one remaining mention is a historical
  comment, unaffected.
- [x] **Q109 useActivityBadgeCounts' debounce timer didn't re-check visibility
  at fire time.** `scheduleLoad()` checked `isHidden()` only when a wake-up
  arrived; if the 400 ms timer started while visible and the tab was hidden
  before it fired, `loadCounts()` still ran both reads while hidden —
  contradicting the file's own comment ("nobody can see a badge on a hidden
  page"). Fixed: the timer callback re-checks `isHidden()` and defers to
  `onVisibility` the same way the schedule-time path does. Guard:
  src/test/hotQueryLoad.test.ts's new behavioural test ("re-checks visibility
  when the timer fires…", fake timers + controllable
  `document.visibilityState`, 1 @mutate), proven red on the unfixed code (1
  read while hidden instead of 0), green on the fix.

- [x] **Q102 The admin credential queue can show a row with no Approve or
  Reject.** `get_pending_credentials` lists any helper_credentials row in
  unverified/submitted, but AdminCredentialQueue renders the actions only
  when that row has a document (license_status pending AND license_url).
  RLS lets a member INSERT a helper_credentials row with no document_url, so a
  pending row can sit in the queue with nothing for an admin to act on
  (prod's seeded one still does outside a messy-input run, until prod-seed.mjs
  is re-applied). Either require a document server-side or
  render the row as "no document yet". DONE 2026-09-23 (20260923101130):
  VALIDATED CHECK helper_credentials_pending_review_needs_document (a
  trade_license/insurance row in unverified/submitted needs a non-blank
  document_url; background_check stays exempt because stripe-webhook inserts
  it 'submitted' with no document), and get_pending_credentials() reads only
  documented rows, reports 'pending' only with a document (either store) and
  lists a person only when one credential is actionable. Prod violators before
  the change: 0 of 3 rows. Guards: src/test/pendingCredentialNeedsDocument.test.ts
  (7 @mutate, all killed; 5 of 7 red on the chain without the migration) and
  src/test/pglite/pendingCredentialNeedsDocument.pglite.mjs (3x apply, ALL
  PASS; NEW_MIGRATION=skip -> 8 FAILED). Needs an lh-authz-rls REVIEW-ONLY pass.
- [x] **Q106 A signed-in client can log error rows as a guest (MEDIUM, review
  of 469cf4e3f, 2026-09-23).** Live policy anyone_can_insert_errors checks
  `user_id IS NULL OR user_id = auth.uid()`, so an authenticated session may
  insert user_id NULL. trg_error_logs_zz_user_error_screen then passes NULL to
  ops_alert_record_user_error_screen, which applies the guest repeat cap (20)
  instead of the per-account cap (5) and spends the shared guest budget. Fix:
  stamp_error_log_origin (BEFORE INSERT) sets NEW.user_id := auth.uid() for
  role authenticated, so a signed-in client cannot erase its identity; guard
  red on the unstamped definition. DONE 2026-09-23 (20260923094457, from the
  live pg_get_functiondef): stamp_error_log_origin sets NEW.user_id :=
  auth.uid() for role authenticated (still SECURITY INVOKER). The only client
  writer, src/lib/errorLogger.ts, sends the id from the localStorage session
  blob or null when it cannot read one; either way the row is now the JWT's.
  Guard: src/test/errorLogClientIdentityAndThrottle.test.ts (@mutate);
  behaviour: src/test/pglite/userErrorScreenRepeatCap.pglite.mjs (auth insert
  with user_id NULL -> stored as the caller, ledger count 5, was 20).

  BEFORE/AFTER (measured): sweep_silent_cron_failures cron run 2,676 ms
  (08:47Z) -> 412 ms (09:47Z); its detect query 2,552 ms -> 20 ms (EXPLAIN

  meaningful: load is set by which CI suites are running (baseline 32 calls/s

  ms/s, app p95 7.1-12.9 ms, during a press run + push suites). Live monitor
  verified: samples every 5 min since 09:20Z, all clean; prod-errors run
  35845088930 fed "0 statement timeouts in 60 min" (workflow sample stored).
  Near-miss worth knowing: 09:40Z had 14 active backends (threshold 15) and
  85-87% connections during CI bursts. Owner compute-tier decision: NOT raised
  now — no breach since the restart; the monitor will say if one comes.
  Hypothesis for 09-22 (unproven): sustained CI browser load on a small
  instance; Q60/Q104 measure the ceiling.
  CORRECTIONS to the evidence below (measured 2026-09-23 ~09:00Z):
  "client polling" was not polling — src/ has one refetchInterval (admin
  broadcasts, 15 s) and three gate-screen timers. The applications count and
  get_my_pending_direct_offers are the nav badges, fired on every page load
  TWICE (MobileNav and DesktopSidebarNav both mount useActivityBadgeCounts;
  edge logs 1.89-2.0 offer RPCs per user_blocks request) and on every
  realtime wake-up. Of at least 112,356 such requests in the 24 h to 09:00Z,
  105,824 (94%) came from CI preview builds (referer 127.0.0.1:4173, mostly
  HeadlessChrome from Azure), most of the rest from other localhost ports, 690
  with no referer, and 687 (0.6%) from louisianahelpr.com. The "29 s CI live check" is not CI: 2 calls total, as
  postgres, no source in the repo (scripts/check-live-privileges.mjs runs a
  different query) — an ad-hoc information_schema.column_privileges read. The
  "256 statement timeouts after the restart" were the outage hours: postgres_logs
  shows 0 from 16:00Z 09-22 to 09:10Z 09-23.
  EVIDENCE GATHERED 2026-09-23 ~05:40Z (Supabase logs + pg_stat_statements):
  - Timeline: 08:00-15:00Z, 22-42 "canceling statement due to statement timeout"
    per hour plus connection/SSL resets; then at 15:00 "the database system is
    shutting down" / "terminating connection due to administrator command".
    pg_postmaster_start_time = 2026-09-22 15:23:42Z: a RESTART ended it.
  - What timed out was NOT app SQL: 92 PostgREST schema-cache introspections,
    47 `pg_timezone_names`, ~60 postgres_exporter metrics queries. Trivial
    catalog reads starving means the INSTANCE was resource-starved, not one bad query.
  - Load since the restart (14h, 1.46M statements, ~29/s, 30 of 60 connections
    in use, pre-launch with no real users): realtime WAL polling (97k calls,
    897s) + publication scans (186k); client polling of applications+jobs
    (30k, 310s); storage.objects lookups (41k, 249s);
    get_my_pending_direct_offers (32k); OUR OWN CI live check (column
    privileges query, 29s EACH); sweep_silent_cron_failures (2.4s each).
  - HYPOTHESIS (not proven; no CPU/memory graphs available through these
    tools): the smallest compute tier + constant realtime/polling load +
    prod-hitting sweeps + dozens of migration deploys on 09-22 (each forcing a
    PostgREST schema reload, 92 of which timed out).
  - TO DO: (a) monitor saturation BEFORE it tips over: statement-timeout rate
    from postgres_logs, connection use as a % of max, and slow-query p95, into
    the alert ledger; (b) cut the load: audit client polling (refetchInterval
    etc.), realtime subscription count, the 29s CI privileges query, and the
    2.4s cron sweep; (c) OWNER DECISION: compute size vs the free tier (a paid
    upgrade is the owner's call; see memory free-tier-no-paid-upgrades).
  Original item: ROOT-CAUSE the 2026-09-22 database outage. 457 pg_cron jobs never
  started (08:00-15:00Z "job startup timeout"); uptime reported "database: no
  answer in 10000ms"; profile loads timed out (the owner saw the error screen at

  alerted, but WHY is unknown: free-tier resource limits, connection

  note). In zod 4.5.4 (node_modules/zod/v4/core/schemas.js) the probe is
  `jit && allowsEval.value`, so `z.config({ jitless: true })` at app start
  short-circuits it. Do: set it once in the entry file before any schema
  parses; prove it by counting CSP reports on one page before/after; add a
  guard that the config call stays (and that nothing re-enables JIT).

  review of Q50, 2026-09-23).** (1) The under-refund ceiling skipped every job

  helper_id IS NOT NULL, not commitment (live pg_get_functiondef); fixed by

  has never cancelled a job) but 6 seed jobs were in the reachable state;

7. **Turn off Supabase's GitHub auto-deploy to production (Q121). Top priority.**
   On every push to main, Supabase's GitHub integration applies new migrations
   (and deploys edge functions) to prod within about a minute. Our deploy
   workflow's safety checks (lint, replay test, destructive-DDL stop) run after
   that, so for the last 24h (24 migrations) they have not blocked anything.
   Measured from prod's own logs 2026-09-23 (Q117). What you do: Supabase
   dashboard > Project Settings > Integrations > GitHub, and switch off
   automatic deploys to the production branch (exact toggle name not verified).
   Until then, each new migration turns db-deploy red by design (the new
   provenance check), so the side channel stays visible.

7. **Backups (Q45): the restore is proven weekly now; five things are yours.**
   **ANSWERED 2026-09-23 (owner pop-ups): (a) yes, Pro is paid and intended; (b) keep 14-day retention; (c) back up uploaded files EXCEPT id-documents, encrypted (work item Q147); (d) no PITR (no paid upgrade); (e) BACKUP_PASSPHRASE and ban_fingerprint_salt go in the owner's password manager (owner to-do).**
   Measured 2026-09-23. Nothing below is needed for the drill to stay green.
   (a) **Is the Supabase org really on Pro?** The API says `plan: "pro"`, and
   `supabase backups list` shows 7 daily platform backups. Every doc said
   "free tier". If you are paying $25/mo, you already have in-place daily
   restores for 7 days. If you did not mean to be on Pro, that is a bill.
   (b) **Backup retention is 14 days, not 90.** The repo caps artifacts and
   logs at 14 days (`maximum_allowed_days` 90). Raising it to 90 is one
   setting, but it lengthens EVERY workflow's artifacts, and on the free
   GitHub plan that can go past the artifact-storage quota (billed). The
   alternative is a monthly copy somewhere else. Your call.
   (c) **Storage files (photos, ID documents) are in NO backup**: 152 objects,
   about 10 MB (proof-photos 64, job-photos 61, message-attachments 18,
   user-documents 4, avatars 4, id-documents 1). The platform backup excludes
   them too. Backing them up needs a service-role key as an Actions secret and
   puts ID documents (encrypted) into a GitHub artifact: a privacy trade-off.
   (d) **PITR** (restore to the minute) is a paid add-on, about $100/mo plus
   Small compute. Without it, worst-case loss is about 24 h (platform) or
   24 h + GitHub's up-to-7 h schedule delay (ours). You said no paid upgrades,
   so this is only the measured gap.
   (e) **Two secrets that no backup holds.** `BACKUP_PASSPHRASE`: is there a
   copy outside GitHub? If not, losing the GitHub account also loses every
   backup. `ban_fingerprint_salt` (vault): it cannot be re-created, and
   without it every stored ban fingerprint stops matching after a restore.
   Want it (encrypted) in the backup, or kept in your password manager?

- [x] **Q218 DONE 2026-09-23: migration 20260923172145 (not yet deployed; branch cloud/q207-q218-cron). sweep_cron_http_failures now lists ACTIVE cron.job rows whose command has 'net.http_post(' but not 'cron_http_tag(' and files one error_logs row per job per UTC day (source 'cron-http-untagged'), named in its Slack post; sweep_silent_cron_failures (restated from 20260923090536) ingests only responses whose request id is in cron_http_requests, so a manual probe is no longer a cron run. GUARD: src/test/cronHttpRequestsAreTagged.test.ts (each new @mutate proven red). Follow-up: Q287 (ledger close rule). Was:** An HTTP cron without the request tag is invisible to the failure sweep (Q174 review, 2026-09-23).** Since Q174, sweep_cron_http_failures only sees responses whose request id is in cron_http_requests. A cron added from the dashboard/SQL editor, or re-set by a later `cron.alter_job(command := ...)` without `cron_http_tag(`, would fail silently. Fix: the sweep also lists active cron.job rows with `net.http_post(` but no `cron_http_tag(` and files one error_logs row per job per day (source cron-http-untagged), included in its Slack post; prove red with an untagged job in the PGlite proof. Same class, sibling: sweep_silent_cron_failures copies any net._http_response with `"fn":"..."` into cron_run_log without joining cron_http_requests, so a manual probe counts as a cron run; join it too. Guard to add: extend src/test/cronHttpRequestsAreTagged.test.ts.

Dead AhaEvents deleted: profile_completed, first_payout_received, error_shown, app_crashed. job_completed is a KEY_EVENT (ground truth: completed jobs, both parties stamped, no dispute, not group, non-seed; the 24h sweep and dispute paths complete with no client so they are excluded; SQL not yet run on prod).   LIVE 2026-09-23 (read-only): prod rpc_decide_dispute(uuid,text,jsonb) still does `UPDATE public.jobs SET status=_new_job_status, dispute_resolved_at=now(), dispute_status='resolved'` in the decision transaction, and sends both parties 'Dispute resolved'. execute-dispute-split never writes jobs.status, and its comment at supabase/functions/execute-dispute-split/index.ts:962 wrongly says the decision 'leaves the job disputed until this function flips it'. Money is NOT moved wrongly today: _shared/unsettledDispute.ts blockers stop release-payout, process-scheduled-payouts and void-cancelled-payments on a decided, unsettled dispute. The defect is status and copy that claim a settlement that has not happened. Design: the decision touches disputes only; an AFTER UPDATE trigger on disputes moves the job (completed, or cancelled for a full refund) when execution_status becomes 'executed', which covers both execute-dispute-split and rpc_settle_dispute_without_payment in one place. Layers to re-read: rpc_decide_dispute, execute-dispute-split, rpc_settle_dispute_without_payment, the 7 edge functions that call or check it (create-payment Quick Release gates on jobs.status='disputed', so the claim_dispute_settlement lock becomes load-bearing), and the notification copy. Too big for a quick item: needs an opus lane plus lh-money-escrow review.
  DONE 2026-09-24, no behaviour change, guard added: MEASURED that no platform code path can move a decided-but-unexecuted dispute's escrow except execute-dispute-split. Layers read: live pg_get_functiondef of rpc_decide_dispute, claim_dispute_settlement, open_dispute_as, rpc_withdraw_dispute, rpc_supersede_dispute_decision, rpc_settle_dispute_without_payment and every SQL writer of jobs.payment_status (only redeem_gift_card and the no-payment settle, neither touches held escrow); all 62 active cron.job rows; edge source of every Stripe money call. Per path: auto-release-payment selects live statuses only and hands off to release-payout; release-payout, process-scheduled-payouts, void-cancelled-payments, create-payment cancel_escrow and admin_refund_general run checkUnsettledDispute (fails closed) before any money call; create-payment admin_release_dispute/admin_refund_dispute need status='disputed' plus claim_dispute_settlement, which refuses a decided-unexecuted dispute; auto-resolve-disputes needs status='disputed' plus a 'sweep' claim; instant-payout, cash-out-credits, charge-recurring-visits, onboarding fee and chargeback clawback do not touch a job's held escrow; money-reconciliation only reads. Live: 0 decided disputes with execution_status <> 'executed'. Moving the job transition into execute-dispute-split was rejected: it would change what the UI, money-reconciliation (Q336) and batch RPCs see and add no money safety. The status and copy problem ('resolved' shown before money moves) is filed as Q344. GUARD: src/test/decidedDisputeEscrowOnlyMovedBySplit.test.ts. - [x] **Q267 A retried job post can create a SECOND job (found by Q68, code read 2026-09-23).** src/pages/postjob/useJobSubmit.ts:431 inserts `jobs` with no idempotency key and there is no unique index to stop a repeat (only jobs_one_visit_per_series_date, recurring visits only). If the insert's response is lost, the error path clears submittingRef and sets no cooldown, so pressing again inserts a second row. In the post+pay flow, a lost create-payment response makes handleSubmit delete the first job (cleanupOrphanJob) and a retry posts a NEW job with a new Checkout Session; the first session (key `escrow-<old jobId>`) is never expired. Fix needs a client-generated key sent with the insert and a unique index on (customer_id, key) (data-model + money: lh-money-escrow + lh-silent-failure review). Proof: slow-network post·drop and pay-start·drop (e2e/slow-network/slow-network.spec.ts) go green.
  STATUS 2026-09-23 (cloud/q267-q270-retries): FIXED IN CODE, [~]. Cause: no idempotency key on the jobs INSERT, and a lost create-payment response deleted the job and posted a new one. Fix: useJobSubmit mints one key per post attempt (attemptRef, reset when the details change) and sends it as jobs.client_request_id; migration 20260923181707_idempotent_job_post_and_message_send adds UNIQUE (customer_id, client_request_id) WHERE NOT NULL and re-syncs the column-level SELECT grants (sync_jobs_select_grants); a 23505 on the key reads the existing job back (findJobByKey) and carries on; a create-payment failure ON THE WIRE (isNetworkFailure, src/lib/networkFailure.ts) keeps the job and the retry pays for that same job (create-payment already retires the prior session); a server refusal still deletes it as before. Layers read: client write (useJobSubmit), RPC/edge (create-payment re-mint gate + session expiry), DB (jobs indexes, INSERT/SELECT grants, jsonb_populate_record SETOF jobs RPCs unaffected), reader (JOB_READABLE_COLUMN_LIST). PGlite 3x: old state 2 jobs for one retried request; after, the retry is 23505 and authenticated can SELECT the key. GUARD: src/pages/postjob/retryAfterLostResponse.test.tsx (7 @mutate lines, each killed; 6 of 7 original tests red on the pre-fix file, incl. a job starting within the hour whose expires_at floor changes per press; LANDING 2026-09-23 added Q267 (d): if openExternalUrl throws, the attempt key is kept so the next press pays for the same job — red when the key is cleared before the redirect, as on the branch) + e2e/slow-network post·drop and pay-start·drop. Noticed, not changed: the SETOF jobs RPCs get_jobs_for_my_applications / get_my_pending_direct_offers also hand client_request_id to applicants; it is a random per-attempt key that grants nothing (a lookup by it is still RLS-scoped, and an INSERT is pinned to the caller's own customer_id), so judged harmless. NOT YET SEEN LIVE: no prod access in this session, so the migration is not deployed and slow-network.yml has not run on it.   STATUS 2026-09-23 (cloud/q267-q270-retries): FIXED IN CODE, [~]. Cause: the retry's "Already applied to this job" refusal was shown as an error. Fix: useApplyFlow remembers jobs whose last apply failed ON THE WIRE (isNetworkFailure only; a 500 or other server answer is a known failure), with the time the first such attempt started (outcomeUnknownJobIds). On the retry an already-applied refusal (isAlreadyAppliedRefusal, applyErrorCopy.ts) counts as success only after reading the row back: status pending AND created_at at/after that first attempt (30s clock slack); attachments then patch THAT row by id. LANDING REVIEW FIX 2026-09-23: the branch accepted any "Already applied" after any unrecognised error, and apply_to_job (live pg_get_functiondef) counts rows of ANY status, so an old rejected application turned into a false "Application sent", a rate-limit bump and an attachment overwrite of the old row. Reachability: withdraw DELETEs the row (AppliedJobsTab confirmWithdraw), so no withdrawn rows exist (live: accepted 94, pending 74, rejected 2); the Browse feed/map hide any job with any application row (useDashboardData appliedJobIds, no status filter), but the ?quickApply deep link (JobDetail, notifications) opens the apply sheet without consulting appliedJobIds, so a helper with a rejected row can reach Apply. No DB change (UNIQUE(job_id, helper_id) already held). FIXED f74a8ce65 (owner decision 2026-09-23: batch, free, up to ~20 min later): vercel.json `git.deploymentEnabled: false` (a push deploys nothing); .github/workflows/prod-deploy.yml (push + every 20 min + dispatch) runs scripts/prod-deploy.mjs, which skips when prod is at main HEAD, when no deploy path changed since prod's commit, or when the newest production deployment is < 20 min old, else creates ONE production deployment of main HEAD via the Vercel API and waits for READY (fails closed -> nightly-red; in main-red-watch and schedule-heartbeat). prod-freshness served-commit now runs hourly, not on push, and demands shipping commits pushed >= 45 min ago. Live 2026-09-23: 11 commits pushed 17:49-18:08Z (incl. f74a8ce65, which changed vercel.json) created 0 Vercel deployments (list_deployments since 17:48:50Z); push run 35900420421 then created exactly one, dpl_6QXCvRALMm1RKAfxwmWc5ZYaPdTV, READY at main HEAD 5b466d317, and www serves build-commit 5b466d317; dispatch run 35900861088 skipped ("no deploy path changed since 5b466d317"). - [x] **Q291 ops_alert_verify can be starved by volume (found by the Q64 authz review, 2026-09-23; pre-existing).** It re-asks at most 200 open items per run, newest `last_seen` first (20260923050059), so a burst of fresh items (measured in PGlite: 250 report items) means an older money item such as detect_stuck_payments is never re-checked (verify_started_at stays NULL) until the burst clears. Q64 now caps user-report items at 20 new per hour; the verifier itself should be fair (e.g. ORDER BY verify_started_at NULLS FIRST, then last_seen). Guard when fixed: a PGlite case with 250 fresh items and one old cleared item that must close.

- [x] **Q293 A scheduled prod-load run can be cancelled with nothing reported (found by the Q70 review, 2026-09-23).** GitHub keeps one PENDING run per concurrency group; a third run entering prod-load cancels the waiting one at workflow level, so no job (not even notify) runs and no nightly-red issue is filed. For the monthly privacy journey that is a missed month. schedule-heartbeat.yml derives each workflow's cadence from its cron (weekly here, because of the first-7-days gate), so it cannot tell that the one DUE week was cancelled. Fix: have the privacy journey's green notify record the month it ran (e.g. the nightly-red issue sync, or a dated marker), and alarm when the current month has none after day 8. Guard: a test on that check.

- [ ] **Q307 Notification templates check WHO sends, not WHETHER the event happened (Q223 follow-on, 2026-09-23).** Only revision_requested, application_declined and no_show_reported read the database to prove their event (a job_revisions row, a rejected application, a no_show strike). The other nine are gated to the right side of the job but not to its state: the assigned Helpr can send "Dispute withdrawn" on a job with no dispute, and the poster can send "Dispute resolved ✓ … Payment will be released" (type payment) before resolving. The words are all server-built and true-shaped, so the harm is a misleading notice about the sender's own job, not a phishing primitive. Fix: a state predicate per template in supabase/functions/_shared/notification-templates.ts (dispute_status, arrival / working stamps, poster/helper confirmation stamps), or move each notice into the RPC that performs the transition. Guard: extend src/test/edge/create-notification.test.ts with one "state not reached → 409" case per template.
